EXPLANATORY MEMORANDUM 


. In the light of revision of employco s subscription /emp 
loyer s share of contribution to the Employees Provident Fund 
from 8 - 1 / 3 per cent to 10 per cent the Government of India 
have also decided to enhance the employces sub cription 
employor s share of contribution to tho CPF from the exist 
ing 8 -13 per cent to 10 per cent w . e.f . 1 - 3 - 1992 . It is certified 
that none of the employees governed hy these rules will be 
adversely affected with this amendment. 


2 . अभिदायी भविष्य निधि नियम ( भारत ) , 1962 
के नियम 8 के उपनियम ( 1 ) में , उपखंड ( ख ) के स्थान 
पर निम्नलिखित खंड रखा जाएगा, अर्थात : - - 
"( ख ) वह इस प्रकार व्यक्त की गई कोई भी रकम हो 

सकती है जो उसकी उपलब्धियों से अधिक और 
उपलब्धियों के दम प्रतिशत से कम नहीं होगी । "

का . आ . 252. - केन्द्रीय सरकार दिल्ली विशेष पुलिस स्थापन 
अधिनियम , 1946 ( 1946 का 25 ) की धारा 3 द्वारा प्रदत्त 
शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए एतद्वारा निम्नलिखित अपराधों 
को ऐसे अपराधों के रूप में विनिर्दिष्ट करती है जिनका 
दिल्ली विशेष पुलिस स्थापन द्वारा अन्वेषण किया जाना है 
यथा : -- - 
( क ) पराक्राम्य लिखत अधिनियम , 1881 ( 1881 

का 26 ) की धारा 138 के , अन्तर्गत दण्यनीय 
अपराध, और 
ऊपर वणित किसी एक या अधिक अपराधों और 
वैसे ही संव्यवहार को अनुक्रम में किए गए किसी 
अन्य अपराध या अपराधों के संबंध में या उनसे 
संसक्त प्रयत्न , दुष्प्रेरण और षडयंत्र ।

9 . But the manaşement has sought to justify its action by 
Government which is contravention of Sec 25F of the ID . taking the stance that the initial appointment and his 
Act. The services of more than 70 workmen who were em continuance in service by the Asstt. Manager ( D ). Food

Storago Depot, Josidih , as casual workman was illegal and 
so it shall be deemed that he was never engaged at all. In 
support of this contention the case of Bijoy Kumar Bharti 
& others Vs. State of Bihar ard others reported in 1983 
Lab . I. C . 1884 (Palnu ) has been cited , I am constrained in 
stato that no such proposition of law us mentioned by the 
management is enshrined in this decision , The Court has 
held that any workmin upp inted illegally or irregularly hi s 
no right to claim or costile in the post . Thir Lordships 
have not decided that tho provisions of Sec . 25- F of the 
I. D . Act arợ not applicable to such workman, Admittedly , 
the concerned workman was engaged by the F . C .1 , und lie 
worked for more than 240 days in 1986 und 1987 . That 
being so , the management is bound to comply with the 
provision of Şec , 25 - F of the I. D . Act before terminating his 
service by way of retrenchment. Hence , I consider that ilic 
action of the mana "ament in terminating the service of the 
concerncd wurkman without complying with the provisions 
of Sec . 25- F of thc I. D . Act is illegal and unjustified . 

10 . The manugement has taken the stance in its wriiten 
Mtatement that the concerned workman sccured the employ 
ment with the collusion of some interested person who is 
not authorised to appoint or allow continuance of job beyond 
7 days under cmergency condition. It appears from the 
evidence that Rajendra Sinha was thc Depot locharge of 
Josidih at the time when the concerned workman was 
engaged ( Exts . W - M - 6 ). There is not a whit of evidence 
on rccord to indicate that the concerned workman secured 
mployment in collusion with Srl. Sinba, MW - 1 A . K . Roy 
has stated that on enquiry he found that the concerned 
workman used to come once in a while at the Depot and 
used to serve some glasses of water , and that he got infor 
mation ard indication that he was working in the house of 
the Asstt. Manager . The concerned . workman has denied 
these allcgtions. Shri Roy has not provide the source of 
information or indication that the concern : d workorn was 
working in the house of the Asstt. Manager. In the com 
meots provided 10 the Assti, Labour Commissioner ( C ) the 
management has not taken this stance ( Ext. W - 1 ). The 
documents produced by the management do not also support 
the stance of the management. That being so . I consider 
that the statement of Sri Roy with regard to the work 
dono by the concerned workman at the Depot and his work 
ing in the house of the Asstt. Manager is embelishment of 
fact in order to lend succour to the case of the management. 

11 . The evidence of the concerned workman discloses that 
he used to get his wages from the District office , Bhagalpur. 
His rejoinder to the management s written statsment discloses 
that his appointment was known to the District Monages, 
Bhagalpur as the wages were being paid by the District 
Manager , Bhagalpur. This staterierit of fict has remained 
udasgailed . The District Munar. Bhagalpur, it appears , 
disapproval of the engagement of the concerned workman 
as casual workman sometime in th : 1: « t part of October , 
1987 (Ext. W - 

4 M -0 ). 
12 . The concerned workman hos claiviej regularistim in 
service as Cluss IV enployee . In his statement. he has 
stated that his duty roster was at par with regul. - work 
man . He has further stated that during 1988 - 80 many of 
the casual workmen were regularised in service. His written 
statement discloses that they were regularised in service as 
Watchmon . But there is no cvidence on record to indicate 
that any post of Watchman has remained vacant cither at 
Josidih or elsewhere . Hence , his claim for regularisation 
as Watchman is not sustainable . But the management shoull 
try to regularise him in service if and when any vacancy 
in Class IV post arises . 

13 . Accordingly , the following avaril i enrered - the 
action of the management in termitating the service of the 
concerned workman with effect from 21- 1 - 88 is not justified , 
The man igement is directed to re -instate him in service at 
casual workman with effect from the date of his termination 
of service an .1 to pr hin full back wages within one month 
from the date of orolicario .1 of the awarii The management 
should consider his claim for regularisation in service as 
Class IV employee as and when vncan , in such nost arises . 
The concerned wort mon ( Pavan Kumar Ram ) is directed to 
report for dirty within the period prescribed . 
In the circumstarmy of the cose, I award no cost.

REASONS 
7. Issues Nos. 1 & 2 . 

The workman Sliri D . R Naik filed his ufluvit (Ex, W / 5 ) 
in support of his case , and he was cross -txamined on behalf 
of tho company. Shri A . L. , Wagle , the Manager of the 
sunt company, filetl his afidavit (Ex. M /8 ) in support of their 
case , and he was cross -examined on behalf of the workman , 
According to the management of the said compuny. the 
Central Government is not the appropriate Government in 
respect of that coupiny, but that the Governinent of Maha 
rashtra is the appropriate Government, and that the Cential 
( 10 : ernment Industrial Tribunal has no jurisdiction to enter 
talin und deute the present reference . However, the present 
reference nas been made by the Central Government. Furthr, 
the conciliation proceedings were held by the Asst. Labour 
( ommission ( Central). As such , it will be presumed that 
the Central Governmeut in the appropriile Government in 
defect of the company in question , unless some material

By the Order No. L - 120128491-JKB 11 dated

in connection with the dismissal of the aforesaid 
cinslo , el was referred to this court for adjudicat on 
hy the Central Gove ment under Section 10 ( 1 ) ol 
the Industrial Disputes Act. 

2 . Aftor the recoip of the reference order 12 this 
court, pursuant to the notice issued from this court 
both the inaingernent and the worker intercd ap 
perurica through counsels and fiied claim state 
19.*** *s setting forth their stan1 in the dispute . The 
brief averments in the statemen ? filed by the wor 
ker are as follows:- The worker was employed as 
a Part -time Sweeper in the Cherootty Road Branch 
of 117 . + 9nqrement baik from 1975 onwards . For 
reasons best known to the managenient, the manage . 
mayt is alnis in eninical terms with the worker 
with the result th : management used to harras this 
worker often . On 2 occasions the worker was charne 
shreted and in nosod punishments alleging Cerain 
misconducts on hc part. Later 2 charge - shects were 
isted to the worker on 15 - 7 - 1988 allcging that on 
27- 6 - 1998 around 11 . 30 A . M . they worker forced her 
way into the Cabin of the Senior Manager V . K . 
Sivariman and abused him at the ton of her voice 
therahy crealinga an uply scene in the honk promiso 
by the second charge - sheet, she was changed with 
abusing the Branch Manager of the Chorootty Road 


Brach on 14 - 7 - 1988 . In fact th worker is inno 
cont die huis given a reply pleading innocence. But 
111wever the management was pot satisfied with the 
L-xplanation and hence a doniestic enuqiry was con 
ducted by the manageruent in this matter. This en 
quiry was really a farcc as the worker was not given 
any opportunity to defend herself . This enquiry was 
conducted fouting all norms of natural justice. But 
howevur later the Enquiry Officer gave a report to 
the management holding the worker guilty of these 
2 changes and basing on that report the worker was 
disrnissed from Service as punishment by the mana 
gement. This aciion of the management is illegal 
and unjust as the worker has not committed any 
misconduct as alleged . Secondly the finding of the En 
quiry Officer holding the worker guilty is perverse 
and hence basing on that finding the worker should 
not have beet punished at all . Hence an award may 
be passed setting aside this dismissal and directing 
the management to reinstate the worker in service 
with backwages ind continuity of service, 

3. The brielaverments in the strements filed 
by the management are as follow s :Sit is true that 
the woker who is cinploveci as Pari - time Sweeper 
in thc Chorootty Road Branch of the management 
bank was disniissed from service , Bul slle in dimissed 
since she is found to have committed serious miscon 
ducts like abusing the senior Manager and the 
Branch Manager and for disorderly behaviour . On 
provinus 2 Owcasions also the worker was punished 
for similar misconducts. Even then the management 
did not punish the workce straight away and she 
wa punisised only after conducting a domestic en 
quity giving her all opportunity to prove her inno 
cence in the said enquiry . The allegation that this 
enquiry was conducted without giving he reay op 
portunity is falsc . In that cnquiry she was found 
guil; v of oth these charges. Tlil s as the worker is 
found guilty of both the charges in the said equiry 
the management has no other opʻion but to punish 
ber. Since this bring the third occasion in which the 
vorlify is charred for his condict like abing 15 
senior Officers the management has no other aler 
naiitc but to give her only the maximum punishment 
Hence 9.1 award may be passed ratifying the action 
of the lanagement, 

4 . On these ptendings the following points are 
formulated for decision :- - 
( 1 ) Whether the domestic enquiry condecica 

roy the management is valid and legal ? 
( 2 ) Whether the finding on the Enviry Officer 

holding the worker guilty is snstainable ? 
( 3 ) What is the proper punishnicat if any too 

hi awarded to the worker ? 
( 4 ) Result ? 
5 . The evidence consists of the oral testimony wit 
MW1 und WWI and the documents marked as 
Exhs . Mi and M2. . 

6 . Point No . I - - This point pertaining to the 
validity of he domestic enquiry has ann decided hy 
me as a preliminary point by my order dt. 30 -10 - 1992 
holding that the enquiry conducted by the manage 
ment is strictly valid and legal,

7 . Poin No. 2 , - As the enquiry is found valid 
the other question that arises for consideration is 
whether the finding of the Enquiry Officer hold 
ing the worker guilty is sustainable . As stated 
carlier 2 separato charges were given to the 
worker with respect to two different incidents . The 
first charge is that ou 22 -6 - 1988 around 11. 30 AM . 
the worker abused the Senior Ranger Sri V .K . 
Sivaraman forcing her way into the Manager s 
Cabin , and thc second charge is that on 12 - 7 - 1988 
she abused the Branch Manager of the Cherootty 
Road Branch and created a scene in the said 
braich . 

8 . Let me deal with the first charge first. To 
prove this charge the management examined alto 
gether 5 witnesses. The first witness who tendered 
oral evide 100 as MW1 is this V . K . Sivaraman , 
Senior Manager himself , who is said to have 
bren thus abused by the worker on 22 -6 - 1988 
arouild 11. 30 A . M . Hc deposed that on 22 - 6 - 1988 
around 11. 30 A . M . while he was sitting in his 
cabin in the main office the worker forced her 
way into the cabin and started abusing him in Mal 
uyalam at the top of her vocie . He gave a verbaluri 
reproduction of what the worker has said in the 
cabin . Ile further deposed that though he asked the 
worker to cool down the worker continued to abuse 
him in filthy language . MWs 2 and 4 are the two 
customers who were present in that bank at that 
time and they have also deposed that they have 
seen the worker abusing the Senior Manager from 
his cabin at the top of her voice . MW5 is the AC 
countant attached to that branch and he also de 
posed that he has seen the worker abusing the Senici 
Manager . MW 12 is a Clerk working in that branch 
and she has also deposed that she has seen the work 
ker abusing the Senior Manager. Thus it can be 
scen that with regard to this incident these witnesses 
have given a more -or-less identifical and uniform 
version . On going through this version I do not find 
any discrepancy or inconsistency . Further it has 
come out that these witnesses have no axe to grind 
against this worker. So the question naturally arises 
why these witnesses who have absolutely no enimity 
to this worker gave such an evidence against her . 
Thus as rightly pointed out by the Enquiry Officer 
the testimony of these witnesses prove beyond doubt 
that the worker has abused the Senior Manager on 
22 - 6 - 1988 as alleged in the first charge . So much 
so the finding of the Enquiry Officer holding the 
worker guilty of the first charge can be upheld . 

9 . Now coming to the second charge, the said 
charge is that the worker abused the Branch Mana 
ger or the Cherootty Road Branch on 12 - 7 -1988 , 
Thro Sanior Manager who tendered oral evidence as 
MW1 has also spoken with regard to this charge but 
his testimony with resnect to this charge does not 
have much of a relevancy here since he has deposed 
only that with regard to this incident he got a renost 
from the said Branch Manager. The Branch Manager 
who is said to have been thus abused by the worker 
has been examined as MW4 and he has denoced 
that on 12 - 7 - 1988 the worker came to him and told 
him that she could not reach the hark in time as she 
did not pot bus and then he told her that she does 
not have to clean the floor on that day as the staff 
have already taken their seats and then the worker 
271 GI / 93 – 12 


started abusing him in filthy language. This version 
was corroborated by the testimonles of MW6 and 
MW 11 who are the clerical stafts working in that 
branch . They have also deposed that on 12 - 7 - 1988 
the worker has abused at the top of her voice tha 
Branch Manager and created an ugly scene in tho 
the branch , MW 15 is the another worker working 
in that branch and he has also deposed that the 
worker has abused the branch Manager. I see no 
reason at all to disbelieve the testimonies of these 
witnesses since on going through it, it seems to mo 
that they have only spoken the truth . Here also the 
worker has absolutely no case that these witnesses aro 
at logger -heads with her. So the possibility of these 
witnesses giving ſalse cyidence against the workCT 
can be ruled out. What the Enquiry Officer has 
done is that he believed the testimonies of these 
witnesses to hold the worker guility of this 
charge. I too feel that these witnesses have only 
narrated what they have actually seen in the Branch 
Manager s room on 12 - 7 - 1988, and hence their testi 
monies can be fully believed . I therefore conclude 
that the Enquiry officer is right in holding the wor 
ker guilty of the second charge also relying on the 
testimonies of MW4, MW6, MW 11 and MW 15 . 

10 . This point is thus decided in favour of the 
management holding that the finding of the Enquiry 
Officer holdig the worker guilty of the two charges 
is supported by legal evidence and that it is sus 
tainable . 

11. Point No. 3 : - Lastly comes the question of 
punishment. The counsel for the management sought 
to justify the imposition of extreme penalty of dis 
missal stating that on previous two occasions the 
worker was punished for similar misconducts by 
stopping her yearly increments. But even then sho 
did not improve and hence a deterrent punishment 
is called for. The fact that the worker was punished 
previously on two occasions for similar misconducts 
is not disputed by the worker and this fact is more 
or -less concerned by her in her claim statement. 
Thus it can be seen that though she was subiected 
to disciplinary proceedings twice earlier for similar 
misconducts she has not improved . This shows that 
she is incorrigible and this being her nature the 
management is fully justified in dispensing with her 
service as nunishment. But any way the worker being 
a lady in her fifty s who has put in around 15 years 
of service I think this dispensation of service would 
have been in a mi der wav by giving her compulsory 
retirement. I therefore convert the nunishment of 
dismissal given to her by the management into that 
of compulsory retirement, 

12 In the result an award is passed altering the 
punishment of dismissal given to the worker into 
that of comnlusory retirement . 

13 . This award will come into force 30 days after 
its publication in the Official Gazette ,
